She didn’t care if the fairies condemned as a witch for she didn’t love them and only sought to make a country where she belonged. A Britain of her own she could never obtain in Proper Human History.

Over the years, she grew fond of her means. She gained companions she could confide, her composure improved, and she learned to act like the savior she never was. She also changed her name to Aesc (the name given to her by her foster parent), as a more fitting name for her role as savior. But after losing her companions following the fall of Londinium, she created her ideal kingdom.

She originally planned to make her Fairy Kingdom far harsher, but for her sake, she didn’t. Also, even though it is isolated by the furthest end, those from Proper Human History can still visit the Fairy Kingdom because it exists on the same coordinates on the planet. It would have delighted Morgan to know if they enjoyed her kingdom and remembered it forever.

In the present, Morgan still lives and kills Spriggan’s guards. However, she can barely move now. The court fairies contemplate on what to do when Aurora’s voice is heard throughout Camelot.

Calling her an invader from another world, Aurora claims Morgan perpetrated the Calamities and the conflicts between the clans as part of her plan to conquer Britain. She even claims Morgan triggered the last Great Calamity by killing Uther and breaking the harmony between the clans.

She reveals most fairies had perished in the last Great Calamity. She further reveals Aesc is Morgan, and that she had used the World Tree’s magical energy to revive the fairies with a Chaldea-style summoning ritual. The Command Spell the fairies bear is thus something not carved into them at Camelot, but something they were born with. It marks them as slaves, offering its accumulating magical energy and time to Morgan.

Calling Morgan a false monarch who came from Paradise to subjugate them, Aurora asks the fairies of Camelot who their true enemy is, and ends her broadcast.

Swayed by Aurora’s words, the court fairies start to revolt; Spriggan takes this chance to flee. The court fairies then brutally kill Morgan as she begs someone to take her to the throne, not wanting to lose Britain again.

The heroes hear the tolling of the bells when a Round Table soldier comes with a report from Cnoc na Riabh’s army.

Cnoc na Riabh has reached the throne and rung the bells in triumph. Morgan has been executed by Aurora’s knights before the throne. Cnoc na Riabh’s army has detained Morgan’s officials and seized the throne. Furthermore, for his contribution to Morgan’s execution, Spriggan shall be appointed interim chief of the six clans. Morgan’s corpse is damaged to be unrecognizable, so there will be no next generation.

Currently, the Queen’s Soldiers are surrendering. The Round Table Army is to gather before the throne once their wounds have healed. Though Artoria couldn’t claim the throne, the rebellion has nevertheless triumphed. Now everyone needs to prepare for the coronation of the new queen.

A flashback reveals Baobhan Sith was once a kindhearted fairy who worked tirelessly to help others. The fairies had worked her to death many times, yet she forgave them each time she was reborn. Morgan found her each time she was brought to the brink of death, lamenting it because Baobhan Sith was the only one who ever thanked her.

Baobhan Sith died for the final time in Darlington with her soul exhausted and no next generation. Morgan resurrected her and told her to live heinously and cruelly because otherwise she cannot live. She adopted Baobhan Sith and made her her successor, hoping she could have a happy life for once.

Unfortunately, Morgan’s wish for Baobhan Sith never came to be. Baobah Sith confesses she hates fairies because they always abused her. She also laments she couldn’t make a single friend because she was always filthy. But there was one fairy she could talk with normally, even though she hates all fairies. She can no longer remember that girl’s name, the city’s name, or what happened back then, however.

In the present, a mutilated and nearly dead Baobhan Sith prays to Cernunnos, the only being Morgan feared, before she dies and is tossed into the Great Pit. Cernunnos' eyes glow faintly brighter.

INTERLUDE I

In the Storm Border’s control room, Goredolf tells the crew they must now choose whether to withdraw or send in additional forces to conquer the Lost World. He then has Nemo restart the control room and reveals they just received word Morgan is dead.

In recognition of Chaldea’s deeds, the fairies will gift one of Camelot’s Rhongomyniads. The delivery will take seven days, so Chaldea will remain for another week. With it no longer necessary to converse power, Goredolf orders the crew to make ready to receive the Holy Lance.

Yet in spite of all this good news, Holmes knows nothing is settled yet. They still don’t know the Lostbelt’s origins, why Camelot was built, the purpose of the Pilgrim Bell’s ritual, nor the mission of the Fairies of Paradise. They do not know where the Red and Black Calamities, the Great Calamities that destroyed Britain in the “first” Fairy Calendar, disappeared to. The identity of the original adversity who sought to destroy Britain is also unknown.

Holmes feels an unnatural sense of malice throughout Da Vinci’s report and suspects the truth is elsewhere. So, while he agrees with restoring power and regaining their spirits, he concludes it isn’t yet time for them to return home. He requests they be put on alert for the worst-case scenario, stating this where Chaldea is needed—the battle to save the world.

Three days later, Artoria’s party has returned to Salisbury. Barghest has stayed behind in Camelot to serve as Cnoc na Riabh’s proxy in negotiations with the upper-class fairies. Percival also stayed behind to oversee the acquisition of Rhongomyniad. It will take a total of four days to remove the Holy Lance and deliver it to the Storm Border.

Da Vinci and Artoria are both worried about Percival’s condition, though he refuses to take a rest. Da Vinci decides to place him in one of the Border’s recovery pods after he gets Rhongomyniad to ease his fatigue.

Cú Chulainn suggests doing the same for Habetrot, having noticed she’s been losing energy ever since Morgan died. Now she won’t leave her bed and wakes up only to write in her journal.

Muramasa reconfirms with Artoria her decision to hand the throne over to Cnoc na Riabh, whose coronation will be held in the city’s cathedral, and return to Tintagel. Artoria decided thus because she failed to defeat Morgan herself and she doesn’t have a mind for politics.

After the heroes toast their victory, Da Vinci states with Morgan gone there is no further threat of invasion into Proper Human History. The Fairy Kingdom is at peace, at least until the bleaching of the Earth is rescinded. Once that happens, the Fairy Kingdom will either continue to coexist with Proper Human History or be rejected by it.

The fairies will not disappear at the very least, for whom Da Vinci is also looking into possibly using the Storm Border for emigration. Artoria and Cnoc na Riabh will take care of things until Chaldea returns. But once Chaldea has taken care of their own errands, Goredolf will return to greet the two of them.

Mike is sad Da Vinci will be leaving after the coronation. But he is happy she is free today to discuss his business plans for the next few years.

Reminded by Ritsuka, Mash recounts her conversation with Barghest before they left Camelot. Barghest said she had put in charge of enforcing the new reform policies. She also told Mash that Aurora and Spriggan said they would follow Cnoc na Riabh. It is concerning hasn’t been word from Muryan, though.

Barghest decried herself as a traitor unworthy to be called a knight. Mash, however, insisted Barghest was still a loyal knight, who only rebelled out of concern for Britain’s future. She knew of one such knight, so it pleases her to see the rebels had accepted Barghest. She continued the strong protect the weak and considered Barghest the magnificent knight Boggart said she was.

Her words made Barghest feel like she could endure whatever punishment she will face. She thanked Mash, calling her a true Knight of the Round Table, and hoped to meet another someday. Mash told her she already met one, revealing to her the knight who stopped her at the farm was Sir Tristan. Barghest is ashamed she failed to recognize one of her idols.

Mash then asked Barghest why she is so attached to the Knights of the Round Table, feeling it is not just because of the Gift Morgan bestowed upon her. Barghest confessed that she admired them because they always came together to defeat evil and protect the people. Their stories helped her, and she hoped it could be the same in the Fairy Kingdom. But she couldn’t do it and instead Mash and her friends did.

She told Mash to tell Ritsuka she is thankful they supported the Child of Prophecy. She owes them a debt of gratitude, so should they ever need her, she shall be by their side wherever they may be.

Cú Chulainn leaves to check on Cnoc na Riabh after reassuring Ritsuka and Mash Barghest will be of great support to Cnoc na Riabh.

In Gloucester, Muryan’s memory has deteriorated a presumable cost of how she achieved her revenge. She does not remember killing the Fang Clan and twice she forgets Koyanskya’s presence.

Koyanskya confirms Morgan is dead and the rebels now control the castle. She further reports the coronation ceremony for the new queen will be held tomorrow. She also tries to warn Muryan her position will be in jeopardy if it becomes known she is why the Fang Clan is missing. Muryan, however, is unaware the Fang Clan is gone.

Returning to the topic of Camelot, Muryan expresses disappointment in Artoria giving the throne up so easily. But she considers fortunate Cnoc na Riabh was chosen as the new queen with the Great Calamity near since, as a direct descendant of Mab, she knows the importance of the throne.

Muryan plans to ally with Cnoc na Riabh, then have her overthrown once the Great Calamity has been abated. Afterward, she will eliminate Chaldea, seeing Proper Human History as an obstacle to the Fairy Kingdom. She also wants to eliminate them because they are Koyanskaya's enemies as well.

She tells Koyanskaya to tell Cnoc na Riabh to remove the Round Table Army from Camelot once she has been made queen and to not give Rhongomyniad to Chaldea, considering them both the property of the Fairy Kingdom. Normally, she would ask Oberon to do it, but he has gone missing.

Koyanskaya agrees to attend the coronation in Muryan’s stead. She then asks Muryan what she intends to do now, concerned how long she has shut herself in her room.

Muryan replies she will investigate the truth of how Morgan protected them from the Great Calamity and the Great Calamity itself. She is certain she can find the truth in one of Camelot’s books, asserting she can undercover anything so long as it’s written in a book.

At her camp, Cnoc na Riabh tells Cú Chulainn that Morgan’s throne is what maintains the cycle of fairies. After completing her pilgrimage, Morgan founded a false Round Table in defiance of her mission. Called the Summoner’s Throne, whoever joins themselves to it becomes the Master of all fairies in Britain. Morgan was desperate and alone, so she summoned the fairies and maintained their lives.

Mab learned this truth during her last decisive battle in Year 400 of the Queen’s Calendar. She, the clan heads, and some upper-class fairies were the only ones who didn’t perish in the Great Calamity at the end of the Fairy Calendar. Mab wasn’t resurrected by Morgan, so she didn’t have to obey her. But neither could she fight her, given how far she went to keep Britain alive.

Thus, Mab and Morgan then formed a secret pact, where the King’s Clan will succeed Morgan if she ever grows weak. Mab afterward retired in Edinburgh and produced an heir for when Morgan grew weak. Cnoc na Riabh was thus born to become the next queen.

Though disappointed by how it all turned out, Cnoc na Riabh claims only her Spirit Origin can reforge the contract with all of Britain’s fairies. Cú Chulainn realizes the Child of Prophecy and “true king” in Ainsel’s prophecy were two different fairies, referring to Artoria and Cnoc na Riabh.

A narration speaks of how a passing meteor eliminated all civilization and took all land as resources. Due to a mistake, everything on the planet disappeared. The narrator is the voice of an island on its deathbed and even now, it resounds with the agonizing breath of someone who outlived death.

Three fairies on their way to Salisbury for the coronation notice a single Moss on a hill looking their way. But as it is only one and far away, they are not scared and continue on their way. After they’re gone, however, that single Moss is joined by many more.

The critical point is nearing. The Moss will outnumber the fairies and thanks to a suitable sacrifice, Cernunnos has awoken.

In the Great Pit, a person with glowing green eyes remarks because no one will take responsibility for the foulness, there can be no cure. They declare at the very least when all this comes to a close, they will offer up something grand.

THE END ~CORONATION CEREMONY~

The next morning, the heroes prepare to head to the cathedral and attend Cnoc na Riabh’s coronation ceremony, having been granted special seats as guest of honor. Habetrot still hasn’t come out of her room, though she insisted it was nothing to worry about and the others to just go to the coronation. Concerned, Mash decides to check on her one last time, but Ritsuka goes instead.

Habetrot remembers Morgan told her, Totorot, because she chose to be a “dream” she can only remain active in the time between “her” arrival in Britain and when “she” is spirited away by the Water Mirror.

Ritsuka enters Habetrot’s room to check up on her. Habetrot insists there is nothing wrong with her and accuses them of suspecting she is hiding something like she is actually a famous fairy. Ritsuka denies that is the case stating they know nothing about her past. Habetrot tells Ritsuka she’d be in danger if even one person knew of her situation, so they shouldn’t try to find out. Nevertheless, she appreciates their concern and says it’s too early to say goodbye yet.

Mike sharpens a knife while thinking about how he can make Da Vinci stay.

At the cathedral, Koyanskaya, Aurora, Spriggan, and other fairy nobles have come to attend the coronation. Percival is also there, instead of Camelot, at Barghest’s insistence. He assures the removal of Rhongmyniad will be finished by noon and delivered to Salisbury by tomorrow night. Da Vinci doubts Chaldea can handle one of Morgan’s Holy Lances, but thinks it’ll make great reference material for a Divine Construct. The coronation ceremony then begins.

Presented by Spriggan, Cnoc na Riabh starts to make her vows as Britain’s next queen. But then her own subjects denounce her as a villain and declare the northern fairies aren’t needed in Britain.

A Wind fairy who joined the King’s Clan claims whoever Cnoc na Riabh shares her blood with becomes her slave.

An Earth fairy claims he and his brother became Cnoc na Riabh’s subjects in hopes of curing their Moss sickness. Instead, their curse was transferred to a fairy with no value and forced to die. His brother took on an uncountable number of curses and turned into a monster, serving as a slave in Cnoc na Riabh’s Giant Corps. Cnoc na Riabh claims those fairies gave their consent and did so for the sake of their brethren.

The Wind fairy then reveals Edinburgh is the transformed corpse of Mab, which even now remains “active”. Mab had consumed the corpses of fairies to expand the city and create a stronger version of herself: Cnoc na Riabh. Her creation is why the northern lands haven’t expanded in 1500 years, which the Wind Fairy calls a betrayal to Britain.

Cnoc na Riabh tries to explain the truth when she suddenly coughs up blood and collapses. Her subjects, the northern fairies, follow suit. Spriggran flees from the scene in a panic.

Panic erupts throughout the cathedral. The Wind fairy calls on the cathedral guard to execute Cnoc na Riabh, saying a northern fairy can’t be queen because there is one much more worthy. The guards proceed to slaughter the northern fairies. Artoria attacks the guards in a rage.

It is revealed Cnoc na Riabh drank poisoned wine, which was mixed into the cups served to her, the northern fairies, and Artoria’s party. Fortunately, Artoria’s party didn’t drink the wine. Cnoc na Riabh knew it was poisoned, but she drank it anyway, believing that she and her closest subjects, the northern fairies, could endure it.

But the enemy knew loyalty was Cnoc na Riabh’s greatest strength and weakness and had prepared for her own clansmen to denounce her. Cnoc na Riabh grows weaker with every fairy who separates from her, so when her clansmen all over Britain denounced her, her power waned severely. As a result, the poison began to affect her and the northern fairies, and she was easily pierced by a guard’s spear.

Seeing Artoria had attacked the cathedral guard, the Wind fairy concludes she and Cnoc na Riabh were working together like she suspected. He calls them both outsiders to Britain and begins to assert someone should kill them both when he is interrupted by Cú Chulainn’s flames. Cú Chulainn tells the former King’s clansmen to leave or die. The three promptly flee.

Artoria holds her dying friend in her arms, confused why her wounds aren’t healing. Da Vinci senses Cnoc na Riabh’s magical energy decreasing as if her Spirit Origin is being reduced little by little.

Cnoc na Riabh’s memories are fading; she can’t remember Artoria’s name or her own mission. It is the result of her sharing everything (her magical energy, her miracles, her intellect) with her subjects. Cnoc na Riabh dies in Artoria’s arms, smiling, knowing even if alone, her friend will always look for that star.

The residents of Salisbury are heard outside trying to purge the northern fairies. Percival suspects the southern fairies will attack Edinburgh with Cnoc na Riabh gone, and the northern fairies will resist to avenge her. He fears Britain will once again plunge into civil war. Mash compares the situation to the disaster of Londinium’s coronation, though she is confused why she said that.

Cú Chulainn urges they escape before the guards outside get in. The guards believe Artoria had plotted Cnoc na Riabh’s murder. Artoria suspects she’ll be executed under the pretense she was colluding with Cnoc na Riabh or rather because she is a Fairy of Paradise.

But Cú Chulainn disagrees and surmises the poisoner was unaware of the Fairy of Paradise’s mission. If they were, they wouldn’t have killed Cnoc na Riabh. She was the only who could have saved the fairies, but they squandered their last hope without realizing it.

Cú Chulain then gives Artoria the choice of either staying with Cnoc na Riabh or proceeding with “what comes next”. He will respect whatever her choice is, at least for now. Ritsuka is confused as the pilgrimage is over.

Coral arrives to arrest Ritsuka and Artoria for the murder of Cnoc na Riabh, which many fairies testified to. But while she wants to imprison them, her guards want to kill Ritsuka, Artoria, and Percival. They ignore Coral’s call for impartiality and move to kill the three, surmising they’re tougher than those Londinium soldiers. Cú Chulainn easily incinerates them despite their mythril armor.

Mash then protects Cú Chulainn from Melusine’s surprise attack. Melusine was sent to neutralize Artoria and the Chaldeans. Coral leaves at her urging, warned to stay indoors for the next few hours lest she be killed with all the fighting throughout the city.

Percival steps forward to fight Melusine, calling Artoria and the Chaldeans his respected comrades. He doesn’t care if they are from different worlds, as they all support each other. He will use all his power to support those wiling to give their lives to follow the right path as he does. That was something he learned in the cathedral’s gardens, told to become a knight worthy of the Spear of Selection to stand in for the Child of Prophecy.

Melusine is very proud of Percival, so she doesn’t want to lose him. She reminds him he already used the Spear twice, once six years and once against Woodwose. She tries to warn what further use will entail, but Percival cuts off, saying he intended to use it against from the start. Melusine leaves, not wanting to fight Percival.

In the air, Melusine is proud Percival has grown into such a splendid knight like she taught him. She knows she is being foolish but laments she has no other choice. Suddenly she hears fairies everywhere, turning into Moss.

Meanwhile, in Camelot, a Round Table soldier reports to Barghest that fights have broken out between the upper-class fairies and the northern fairies in the castle as a result of Cnoc na Riabh’s murder. Another soldier then reports company-sized groups of Moss have appeared from inside the city.

Barghest asks how the dismantling of Rhongomyniad is proceeding. A soldier of hers says the last report was an hour ago. He also tries to report every fairy in the castle has been complaining about numbness when he suddenly turns into a Moss, even though he never touched one.

A black haze emanates from the Great Pit, the sight of which causes Barghest to collapse. She gets back up and realizes the Moss are coming from the Great Pit. She orders the Round Table Army to escape to the plains, while she covers their retreat.

But, after the soldiers depart, Barghest starts to feel the effects of the Curse of the Black Dog, which was previously sealed by her Gifted Name. Despite that, though, she resolves to kill all the Moss for the hope of emigration.

CONCLUSION

The heroes have escaped Salisbury and just in time too, because with the gates now shut, there is no way back in. Mash is worried about leaving Habetrot behind, but Cú Chulainn reassures her she will be fine on her own.

Muramasa concludes even with Artoria suspected of murdering Cnoc na Riabh, war between the northern and the southern fairies was inevitable. He further concludes because the heroes were allied with the northern fairies, they will be labelled as an enemy of the six clans along with the Round Table Army. He suggests Percival head back to Londinium when he notices how pale he is.

Percival insists he is fine, wanting to act quickly upon the situation. But Artoria insists he get treated first, pointing out how irregular his breathing is. He can worry about everything after he gets some rest.

An abnormal number of Moss appear. Muramasa has Mash and Da Vinci escape into the forest with Percival while Artoria, Ritsuka, Cú Chulainn, and himself hold off the Moss for two minutes. After two minutes, they all escape into the woods.

Cú Chulainn’s wolves arrive and report Moss are appearing everywhere, and every city has closed its gates. Worse, fairies consumed by fear are likely to become Moss themselves. The Great Calamity has begun.

Cú Chulainn calls the Great Calamity a rot gathered across Britain for over a thousand years. Britain is flooded with the magical energy of innumerable voices of agonizing death, which the Calamity accumulates to grow in size. Thus, so long as Britain exists, the Great Calamity is inevitable.

The Great Calamity this time is the Dweller of the Great Pit’s awakening and it is the primary reason Cú Chulainn was sent to the Lostbelt. But according to his wolves, there are also signs of the Red and Black Calamities. Those two are out of Cú Chulainn’s jurisdiction, though, so he doesn’t know what form they’ll take.

Mash remembers Aesc said Britain will be destroyed when the Dweller awakens, so she recommends stopping it before that happens. Cú Chulainn agrees and reveals it is related to the Fairy of Paradise’s true purpose. Artoria tries to explain what that means when Da Vinci gets a surprise call from the Storm Border.

Holmes explains the Border’s systems are coming back online now that Britain is falling apart. The Border can now move, so Holmes requests everyone return immediately. Also, with Professor still not fully recovered, Da Vinci will be needed to devise a cruising program for Britain’s magical energy density. The Storm Border will return full functionality six hours after the heroes return.

Wanting to hurry to the Border and not leave the fatigued Percival behind, Da Vinci calls out to the hidden Redra Bbit, who’s been tailing the group. He immediately agrees to take them to their destination.

Meanwhile, Barghest finishes slaying the Moss in Camelot. Unfortunately, the Round Table soldiers have all perished. Barghest wonders if she should’ve devoured them if death awaited them both inside and outside. But even if it is the end of Britain, Barghest still vows to protect Manchester.

As he flees for Norwich, Spriggran confesses he planned to have Cnoc na Riabh killed, but only after the Great Calamity was abated. Morgan proved to him the fairies require leadership, so he is puzzled why “that woman” had Cnoc na Riabh immediately killed. But it suddenly dawns on him she wasn’t thinking at all and she only wants to get rid of everything she dislikes.

In Salisbury, its fairies have become violent fanatics of Aurora. Aurora seems oblivious to her city’s deterioration while Coral is unnerved the former would call it peaceful.

Coral reports the northern fairies all gradually lost strength and collapsed as if in response to Cnoc na Riabh’s death. She reopened the nursery and put the northern fairies left alive under house arrest there. Aurora seems to have forgotten the nursery was an institute for raising human children she had ordered built 16 years ago. It had stood abandoned for six years, so Coral made use of it.

Aurora remembers a grey-haired child whose name she cannot recall. He had to be discarded, though, because he grew up from using the spear. He was only human, so once he looks like an adult, he cannot be the Child of Prophecy anymore.

Coral asks if it was selfish of her to reopen the nursery. Aurora tells her it wasn’t and, in fact, finds it perfect for purging the northern fairies in one fell swoop.

Changing the subject, Aurora asks Coral if Ritsuka and Artoria have been arrested yet for Cnoc na Riabh’s murder. She finds both equally guilty but accuses Artoria of manipulating Ritsuka. As such, she will offer Ritsuka clemency for their crimes, hoping to hear stories about the outside world from them.

Coral asserts there isn’t time for that. Moss continue to increase outside the city, and refugees fleeing from the forests are begging to be allowed entry. Some fairies in the city are also developing symptoms of Moss sickness. More places for treatment are urgently needed, so Coral recommends they use the cathedral for such a purpose after allowing the refugees and admitting them. She also wants the humans of Gloucester and Londinium to assist.

Aurora, however, wants the gates to remain closed and for the human guards to execute any fairy who’s contracted Moss sickness. Coral is disturbed Aurora would order the humans to kill their fellow citizens and she would abandon the refugees outside. But Aurora seems to believe everything will be fine in the end.

In Gloucester, a Wind Fairy reports to Muryan on Britain’s current situation. Muryan understands the Great Calamity has begun. She orders the acceptance of all refugees. Her Fairy Territory is still strong, so Moss will not enter the city. As for the fairies turning Moss inside, she contemplates having the Fang Clan execute them. But ultimately she decides to send the Fang Clan out to the exterminate the Moss in the surrounding forests.

The Wind fairy tries to tell Muryan that the Fang Clan is gone, but Muryan demands she follow her orders. In the meantime, she will continue studying to uncover the truth about the Great Pit. She believes if she can parse the “first prophecy” and “Cernunnos” from the story of Britain’s origin Morgan had kept hidden, she can uncover the Great Calamity’s true nature. The Wind fairy leaves to do as instructed.

Muryan learns an invader fell from a shooting star 14000 years ago. But to her horror, she then discovers it is Britain itself, not Cernunnos, that’s trying to kill the fairies in retribution for their forefathers’ sins. Upon learning this truth, she is mortally wounded by a mysterious individual with a black arm. They thank her for eliminating the Fang Clan, as the Great Calamity wouldn’t have occurred if they had killed the Moss. They also confess to orchestrating the Fang Clan’s massacre of the Wing Clan.

Thanks to Redra Bbit, the heroes reach the west coast in a day and a half. Exhausted and with the carriage about to give way, he urges the others to continue without him. Cutting through the Moss on the beach, the heroes board the Storm Border.

They enter the control room in a hectic situation. A panicked Goredolf tells Ritsuka that the Great Pit is a threat, with abnormal magical signatures being detected from it. According to SHEBA, the Collapse is due to occur in a few hours.

Goredolf relaxes a bit, though once he notices Artoria, Percival, Cú Chulainn, and Muramasa, and requests their aid. But he notices there are a few companions from the reports who are missing. Ritsuka informs him that Oberon and Gareth are gone. As for the northern fairies, Mash informs Goredolf Cnoc na Riabh was murdered at her coronation.

Changing the subject, Goredolf informs the heroes that the Storm Border is currently preparing for a water takeoff sequence. Then on his instructions, Da Vinci goes to help Professor, Mash takes Percival to the infirmary, and Muramasa and Cú Chulainn go speak with Nemo about their upcoming strategy.

He tells Ritsuka to get checked in the infirmary and replenish Command Spells. Afterwards, he wants them to debrief him and Holmes on every aspect of Britain, as they will be storming it in a few hours.

Two hours later, Ritsuka has finished debriefing Goredolf and Holmes on Britain. Holmes understands there are two things that must be done. One is stopping the Collapse, which means investigating the Great Pit and preventing the Great Calamity by eradicating the Moss. The other is Chaldea’s objective of acquiring a Divine Construct. On that subject, Holmes asks Cú Chulainn why he, according to Ritsuka, looked dispirited when Da Vinci mentioned Chaldea acquiring its own Divine Spirit.

Cú Chulainn reveals it’s pointless for Chaldea to acquire Rhongomyniad as Divine Constructs and its wielders are a set. Even if they created their own, Chaldea wouldn’t be able to use it because of certain qualifications the bleached Proper Human History lacks. Holmes realizes Sion knew about this, which is why she was so insistent about Britain.

The alarm suddenly goes off. Nemo Marines report gravitational fluctuations at the Great Pit and fires are spreading from Manchester across Britain. Nemo orders a visual of the Great Pit, but Marine tells him it only comes up pitch black.

Nemo requests a progress report on the cruise program. Marine reports it is already complete and it will take 30 minutes to verify. Nemo decides to prepare the ship for battle and tells Ritsuka to go get Mash and Artoria.

In the infirmary, Da Vinci deduces Percival’s condition is related to him being a substitute Child of Prophecy. She further deduces Aurora adopted him, Woodwose selected him from the nursery, and Melusine trained him in swordsmanship.

Percival admits he and the other human children were raised as a substitute Child of Prophecy in case the real one never appeared 16 years later. Woodwose found him the most talented of all the candidates.

Salisbury housed the Spear of Selection, but Aesc placed a seal so only the sinless may wield. Fairies couldn’t touch it, only humans. To be able to wield it is the minimum prerequisite to being called the Child of Prophecy.

After ten years at the nursery, Percival was chosen by the Spear. He heard a voice in his head telling to “kill the wicked fairies with this spear”. It was also the day the Mirror Clan was massacred. But Artoria is confused as that was six years ago.

Percival explains that Spear expends its wielder’s life to exhibit its true power and reveals he is chronologically sixteen. He was ten when the Spear chose him, hence his appearance, and now he is biologically 26 years old. The 30-year life span of humans is why he never underwent a second growth spurt when he used the Spear on Woodwose. Percival believes he is only alive because he is sturdier and blessed with a longer lifespan compared to other humans.

He apologizes to Artoria for raising the Round Table Army in her name when it was for his own sake. Artoria knows, though, he is forcing himself to act to save Melusine.

Nemo alerts everyone the ship will now prepare for combat. Gravitational fluctuations have been detected at the Great Pit, which Holmes believes to be the onset of the Collapse. Nemo states they will storm Britain in 30 minutes and calls all combatants to the bridges.

Percival decides he will join, believing to witness Britain’s fate is the least he can do to his fallen comrades. His wish was futile, but their wish for a future of coexistence was genuine.

Though it’s only been a day and a half, all of Britain is in flames, presumably because of the Red Calamity.

Chaldea heads for Manchester to evacuate its fairies like Barghest was promised. Flames surround the city but the walls protected it, so the fairies inside should still be safe. Ritsuka and Mash are air-dropped in the Shadow Border, with Meuniere driving, to direct the evacuation.

But when they arrive at the city, they find Barghest has burned down Manchester and killed everyone. Barghest attacks the Shadow Border and starts to devour Ritsuka’s life force, so the group decides to retreat. But then Barghest ignores them and heads for Gloucester with a pack of Black Dogs in tow. Ritsuka and Mash both realize she is the Black Calamity.

After the three return to the Storm Border, Holmes designates Barghest as the Calamity of the Beast. She is the source of the flames spreading across Britain. Walled cities are holding out, but the forests and prairies are all gone.

The smoke from the fires gather in the sky, forming thunderclouds. Barghest sustains herself on the lightning generated, increasing her magical energy with every step. Da Vinci would like to neutralize her before she becomes invincible, but confirming the situation at the Great Pit takes precedence.

Fortunately, Barghest’s advancement is slow as Nemo reports it’ll take her 4–5 hours to reach Oxford, giving enough time to create a countermeasure.

Ritsuka is reluctant to fight Barghest, but Artoria accepts the Barghest they knew is gone for good. She realizes now Barghest’s Gifted Name was a seal to keep her from becoming a Calamity, but it broke because she defeated “Gawain”.

Mash realizes the Curse of the Black Dog the Moss King placed on the Fang Clan was the Calamity. She wonders if Barghest knew she would become a Calamity since she was born, and that is why she strived to become the ideal knight.

Marine then shows video feed of the Great Pit, showing a giant furred creature with horns has risen from the Great Pit. Cú Chulainn identifies it as Cernunnos, the reason he was chosen as Odin’s vessel. Ritsuka recognizes it from the mural.

Because of all the curses emanating from Cernunnos, Holmes considers it suicidal for them to disembark at Camelot now. He deduces Morgan had the Rhongomyniads aimed at the Great Pit to destroy Cernunnos once it rose. Ritsuka suggests they hurry to the castle then, but Artoria says it’s too late.

A shape similar to the Calamity of Norwich extends from Cernunnos towards the castle. Artoria confirms the Calamity of Norwich was just one of Cernunnos’ many “arms”. Then, as the “arm” proceeds to destroy the castle, Marine reports the curses from the Great Pit are expanding and are threatening to consume Britain. Many more “arms” are then produced, so Nemo orders an immediate retreat.

Escape seems impossible, though, as Cernunnos’ “arms“ are nigh uncountable, and the surface is now a sea of curses. But just when the Storm Border is about to be consumed, Koyanskaya arrives in her Beast form and absorbs the curses.

A flashback reveals after she returned to Gloucester from the coronation, Koyanskaya found Muryan bleeding to death. She thanked her for her generosity and swore to repay the favor by inviting her to her Primatosphere when it’s established. She reassured Muryan, who feared she herself will forget, as the Beast of Treasuring she never forgets a debt owed.

Muryan admonished herself for not letting go of her hatred. Because of that, she never realized who the real enemy was, and she was being manipulated the entire time. That is why before she died, she requested Koyanskaya to protect Britain in spite of how sinful fairies are.

In the present, Holmes tells Marine to get a visual of Koyanskaya’s Beast form. Marine tries, but Koyanskya is blocking all attempts. Holmes knows a confrontation with Koyanskaya is inevitable, so he was hoping to get more information on her.

Cernunnos’ curse begins to affect Koyanskya, so Chaldea uses the chance she gave them to escape. Koyanskaya, having absorbed too many curses, withdraws to her headquarters. She realizes Cernunnos’ curse isn’t one directed toward enemies, but a curse of self-destruction directed toward oneself and allies.

As Chaldea flees from Britain’s heartland, Barghest has become a titanic beast and is now destroying cities. Curses continued to flow from Cernunnos as it emerged out of the Great Pit. The fairy villages burned to the ground. The residents of Norwich kill each other over two ships, split between fairies and humans. Those who moved to Oxford in hopes of becoming new nobility were killed by the Moss defenseless without the Fang Clan. Salisbury, despite shutting its gates in an act of self-defense, met a fate more ghastly than any other city. Londinium burned to ashes, with the last of the Round Table Army welcoming the fleeing fairies and repelling the Moss. The fairies of Gloucester disappeared one by one as their city burned.

Seeing the apocalypse unfolding before them, Chaldea realizes there is no stopping the Collapse now. Their only hope the 12 Rhongomyniads were lost when Camelot was destroyed. But Merlin appears and tells them they only need to correct the Singularity that the Lostbelt became.

EPOCH OF CREATION

Merlin reveals he was imprisoned in the Garden, but it is vanished when Morgan died. He has been for free for a while but he was occupied with other preparations. He starts to waver, confessing the him everyone is seeing is an illusion he sent from Avalon, only hearing their voices.

He wants Artoria back in Avalon, knowing she has been hearing its call ever since she rang the bells. It is time for her to fulfill her mission. Once that happens, Cernunnos and the Singularity can be eliminated. Artoria responds that she isn’t qualified enough for that.

Cú Chulainn tells her that doesn’t matter and explains Cernunnos’ curse will weaken if Artoria returns after ringing the bells. He confesses Odin tasked him with delivering Artoria to Merlin, the deity not caring if she was dead so long as she rang the bells. Artoria always knew about Cú Chulainn’s plan for her.

Merlin tells Cú Chulainn that was only the worst-case scenario, which no longer applies. He feels Artoria is ready to fulfill her mission of her own free will.

He reveals Avalon can be reached through the Spirit Cave, which can be entered from Albion’s corpse and will take an hour to traverse. With Britain dying, the road to Avalon is now open. But as the World of Magecraft’s most sacred ground, not everyone can enter. Only Ritsuka (humanity’s representative), Mash (Knight of the Round Table), Artoria (Fairy of Paradise), and Muramasa (Artoria’s servant) may go.

Arriving at the Lake District, Nemo warns Artoria’s party that they only have three hours before they set out.

Guiding the heroes through the darkness, Merlin explains in the Lostbelt, Albion succeeded in making a path to Avalon. Unfortunately, it was too narrow for the dragon, so it died aboveground. In Proper Human History, the Spirit Cave is called the Spirit Tomb, though Albion died at 80km mark. Normally, humans cannot enter Avalon, but the Lostbelt itself is a fairyland.

The heroes’ destination is at 2700km while they’re still at the 80km. Merlin clarifies when he said an hour he wasn’t talking about distance but the time it takes. He explains travel time can be consecutively shortened to bring one closer to their goal. But feeling the scenery is boring, he decides to “turn on lights”.

A crystalline cave suddenly manifests. Merlin tells the heroes to keep going for with each step, they reduce travel time. They are in the Earth’s infosphere, so they reach their destination faster by skipping past vague information. The heroes are confused why Merlin is in Fou’s form but decide it’s best not to bring it up.

Ritsuka and Mash notice the “Insect of the Abyss“ mural. Merlin calls it a reflection of the priestess’ memory and reveals the true story of Britain’s origin. The six fairies had murdered Cernunnos using poisoned wine and made his corpse the foundation of Britain.

Merlin reveals the Great Pit is Cernunnos’ grave and the curses flowing from it are his wrath. Cernunnos and his priestess, the last human, indeed saved the six fairies from the Ocean of Emptiness the Earth had become. The fairies were grateful at first, but grew frustrated at Cernunnos for not creating new land and the priestess for her constant lecturing. They eventually decided to kill Cernunnos, concluding its body would make a nice landmass to inhabit.

Under the pretense they wished to worship Cernunnos, the faires held a feast in his honor. Cernunnos believed the fairies were repenting at last and accepted the feast, ignoring his priestess’ warnings. The god drank poisoned wine and died. Afterward, the fairies captured the priestess, made her immortal, and divided her into several pieces to serve as the origin of future humans. Then, being Sub-Bells, the six fairies created offspring to increase their clans as more and more of Cernunnos’ corpse became land.

But after 100 years, their children started to die, though their numbers never decreased since new generations were always born. Eventually, the fairy civilization was founded, its development giving rise to new fairy variants. But, with their children still dying without reason, the six fairies came to realize it was Cernunnos’ anger towards them. Even with his soul is gone, his corpse even now seethes with rage and curses.

Fearing Cernunnos could resurrect at any moment, the fairies fled from its corpse into the new lands formed from the corpses of their children. But Cernunnos’ grave remained ocean as it refused the fairies’ corpses because of their lingering sins. The water was eventually damned, creating the Great Pit.

Muramasa wonders, though, why Cernunnos came to Earth in the first place. Artoria suspects Cernunnos was sent to punish the fairies, but didn’t because he was a gentle soul.

Reminded by Merlin since it relates to the Lostbelt's origin, Mash recounts how 14000 years ago Sefar was slain by a human wielding Excalibur, the greatest of all Divine Constructs which originated in Britain. But having said that, Mash realizes no one ever spoke of Excalibur as if the concept of “Holy Sword” doesn’t exist in this Britain.

Ritsuka recalls Galatine and Arondight exist in the Lostbelt. Muramasa reveals those are fakes, though, their names taken from Proper Human History’s Knights of the Round Table. Barghest’s Galatine is her horn and Melusine’s Arondight is crystallized magical energy. Muramasa concludes no Holy Sword was ever forged in this Britain.

Merlin reveals the six fairies refused to forge Excalibur out of sheer laziness. As a result, Sefar destroyed all of Earth’s civilizations and landmasses. Feeling something was amiss, the fairies went to check on the surface and saw the consequences of their actions. But even then they refused to admit it was their fault the world ended.

Cernunnos, as the only god to take refuge in Avalon for his priestess’ sake, was sent to make the fairies repent. But the fairies killed him and his priestess. For their sin, they and their descendants were barred from Avalon, for only innocent fairies may enter.

To rectify the mistake of Excalibur never being forged, a fairy from the Inner Sea of the Planet was selected and sent to Earth. The first being Vivian, the second was Artoria. Their mission was to get the six clans to admit their guilt and inherit the Mystery of the Holy Sword’s creation.

The Pilgrim bells indicate the clan leaders acknowledge their guilt. The fairy who rings them all will become the Holy Sword itself. Vivian refused and became Morgan. If Excalibur is forged, the foundation of the Lostbelt will cease to be. Artoria should have all realized when she rang Orkney’s bell.

Coming to the end, Merlin tells Artoria she must face the final dragon, who will test her qualifications to enter Avalon. She passes and enters Avalon with the others.

THE MOMENT A STAR IS BORN

Arriving in Avalon, Merlin explains this one is a mere shadow of the real one taken in by the Lostbelt. It lacks his tower and the lake where fairies gather, and its mana is thinner. It only has the Site of Selection, where the six fairies should’ve forged Excalibur.

Merlin will guide the heroes there, but they’ll need to pass Avalon’s final test. Artoria’s past deeds will manifest as obstacles, starting with “winter” and ending with “spring”. The others need to remove these obstacles so Artoria can move forward. Fortunately, it becomes less and less difficult.

Reiterating forging Excalibur is the Fairy of Paradise’s duty, Merlin states once it is done, the Lostbelt will lose its origin point and human history will be stabilized. But forging Excalibur is also vital to human history because if the Lostbelt were to merge with Proper Human History, it would be a world without Excalibur. The Alien God’s defeat will therefore become impossible, though it cannot stop the Collapse either.

Artoria confesses she knew it was her mission to forge Excalibur after she rang Orkney’s bell. She had actually planned to go to Avalon by herself once Cnoc na Riabh became queen. She also knows Chaldea needs a Divine Construct. Both she and RItsuka have their own respective duties to fulfill, so this is the end of their journey.

As the heroes continue onward, Merlin warns the Calamities will never end unless Cernunnos’ never-ending punishment of the fairies is stopped. Because of Morgan, fairies born in the Fairy Kingdom can emigrate to Proper Human History. The Calamities will thus spread to Proper Human History and cause destruction on par with a small-scale Incineration of Humanity. It is, therefore, imperative they are stopped while they’re still in the Lostbelt.

Merlin reveals Barghest was born as a Child of the Calamity, the reincarnation of the Great Calamity that Morgan averted 1000 years ago. Morgan used the name Gawain as a seal to prevent Barghest from becoming the Calamity. But Barghest reached her limit long before the heroes defeated, and it’s miraculous she lasted so long. Barghest could have never emigrated.

Moving on, Merlin expresses concern about defeating Cernunnos, considering how bloated he is with curses. But Artoria believes Muramasa will be their trump card, recalling he is a god slayer.

She then suddenly disappears, having reached the first obstacle. While she confronts her memories, the others will need to defeat the materialization of those memories so she can continue.

After defeating the materialized memories, Ritsuka gets a sickening feeling. Merlin says that is to be expected from Artoria’s most painful memory. They may never know the details, but by defeating the memory some of those feelings are shared with them.

Artoria reappears, having thought the others disappeared and not her. Merlin explains they need to repeat this process three more times so Artoria can purify herself to enter the Site of Selection.

Artoria’s “winter” memories reveal that the fairies of Tintagel raised her in a stable because she was a Fairy of Paradise. With her Fairy Eyes, she saw everyone’s true intentions even against her own will and understood they feared and hated her. Mostly treated with indifference, she was abused by those who believed in the Child of Prophecy and mistreated by those who hated the Fairy of Paradise.

Even so, her treatment didn’t matter because even as a child, she knew her mission to deliver salvation to the fairies would result in Britain’s destruction. She could bear the fairies’ poor treatment of her if there was a reason, but seeing and hearing everyone’s true intentions even in her sleep was unbearable. The tolling of the bells told her to set out a pilgrimage to save fairies, but she considered it unreasonable.

In her sixth year, while training with the Staff of Selection as a melee weapon, she heard a voice from the Staff. The voice claimed to be Merlin and began teaching Artoria magecraft haphazardly.

After many lessons, Artoria asked “Merlin” why he was teaching her magecraft when the fairies of Britain hated her. “Merlin” told her as a Fairy of Paradise she will in time have many fairies as her followers. When that time comes, she must become a savior befitting the wish everyone has of. She must become a fairy tale-like existence, a nameless king who exists on the edge of the world, bringing hope to everyone across time with her mere existence.

Artoria’s lessons came to an end a year later when the village found out and confiscated her staff until her day of departure when she turns sixteen. The days that followed only got worse.

Her “autumn” memories show how she first met Ector. When she was twelve years ago, Artoria was sent to deliver something to Ector who lived alone in his workshop on Cape Drift. She was immediately infatuated with the workshop. Ector thought she was a thief and threatened to kill her. He lived alone for centuries and everyone in the village feared him.

Ector realized Artoria wasn’t a thief upon learning she was sent on a delivery. Artoria sensed he truly hated fairies. Out of fear he might kill her, she decided to leave the bag and flee back home, having lost her previous excitement. But Ector stopped her, wanting to see her face, and realized she was the Child of Prophecy. He then ran Artoria out of his workshop, telling her to never come back, even throwing his hammer at her.

But Artoria came back, and Ector saw she could touch iron without issue. Eventually, after several more visits, Ector made her his apprentice.

A year into her apprenticeship, Ector had became a renowned craftsman receiving special orders from all over Britain. It was during this time Artoria first met Barghest, who was the first customer to come directly to Ector’s workshop. Artoria tried to welcome Barghest to enhance Ector’s reputation, but Barghest completely ignored her.

Barghest came up to receive the sliver armor Ector spent the last four years making for her. After confirming it fit, she tried to recruit Ector to work in her city. But Artoria, already mad at her, refused to let Barghest take her teacher away from her and tried to get her to leave. Barghest left on her own after telling Artoria to learn some manners. Artoria saw with her Fairy Eyes Barghest wrote her True Name in the sales voucher instead of Gawain and swore to never forget it.

In the autumn of her second year as his apprentice, Ector noticed Artoria kept staring at the jewelled hair ornaments he made. He permitted her to take one to use a pattern to work off of. But Artoria, even though she wanted it, declined because she knew it would be taken away.

Then, on the morning of her sixteenth birthday, the villagers demanded Artoria show proof she was the Child of Prophecy by killing Ector, whom they claimed was an elite guard of Morgan.

Her “summer” memories reveal she didn’t go through with killing Ector. But ousted by her “friend”, Artoria was accused by the villagers of being a false Child of Prophecy. For this “crime”, she is captured and placed in an underground cell to await Woodwose’s execution squad, who will arrive in three days. The village became split between supporters of Morgan and the Child of Prophecy.

A wounded Ector eventually rescued her, giving her a hat and returning her staff to her. He told her to escape before daybreak and towards Salisbury when a commotion is heard outside. He and Artoria saw the villagers set fire to the village and were killing one another.

Though baffled by the scene at first, Woodwose attacked the village in an attempt to capture the Child of Prophecy alive. Artoria tried to go save the village, feeling it wasn’t right to abandon the villagers despite how they treated her. But Ector grabbed her and took her to a nearby forest.

There he told her she was on her own and that she needs to leave now as Woodwose’s soldiers were now after her. He lied he would return to his workshop, but Artoria saw he would die very soon from the injuries he had sustained. He encouraged her to set off, truly believing she is Britain’s savior, and finding she is just like her.

Artoria refuted him and tried telling him she’ll live on the cape and leave when the prophecy turns out to be fake after she turns 17. But her words never reached him, as he was already dead. Artoria quickly overcame her grief as is her specialty and left on pilgrimage after thanking everyone for raising her.

She traveled to Salisbury to try to meet with Aurora, only to be turned away by the rebel army. She performed menial tasks to prove she was the Child of Prophecy, but they still didn’t believe her. They told her they didn’t need a Child of Prophecy because they have Aurora. They also told her no one truly believed in the Child of Prophecy.

Laughed out Salisbury, Artoria then traveled all across Britain, but always she was used and ridiculed. She understood the fairies only enjoyed the rumors about the Child of Prophecy, while the real thing was just a hindrance. With no companions to confide in and hated by everyone, Artoria continued her journey alone.

She went to the Nameless Forest in the hopes she could just be herself. But the Fairy of Paradise is immune to the curse of forgetfulness. So she pretended to have lost her memories and withdrew into the tent for newcomers, feeling empty inside.

It was then another “fairy” was brought in—Ritsuka. They had lost their memories, and they were in shambles both physically and mentally, so Artoria thought she couldn’t leave them alone. But most importantly, if they had forgotten all about the Child of Prophecy, she hoped they could become friends.

While waiting for Artoria to return from her “spring” memories, Ritsuka and Mash both express unease at facing more memories as they only bring heartache. Merlin tries to put them at ease by explaining “spring memories” are comprised solely of what that person enjoys.

He confirms Muramasa’s suspicions that Artoria will disappear in exchange for the establishment of the concept of the Holy Sword. Artoria’s accumulated memories, experiences, her Spirit Origin, and even her destiny will all serve as materials to forge Excalibur. She came to Avalon knowing full well this would be the outcome.

Artoria then returns from confronting her “spring memories earlier than expected. She acts like nothing is wrong and races toward the Site of Selection.

Ritsuka is upset about how carefree she is being. Muramasa is relieved they feel that way, but tells them to leave what happens afterward to their elders. He does not clarify by what he means, only recommending they catch up to Artoria.

In her inner monologue, Artoria confesses she sees and hears the world in a storm of malice, whether she is awake or not. It became stronger after her experiences in Tintagel and getting to know the hearts of fairies. It made feel completely lost.

She thought Morgan would have never gotten lost in the storm since she had the conviction to make Britain her own. But she herself hated Britain and its inhabitants, so she couldn’t possibly find her conviction the same way. It was already shown to her, though, when she rang the Pilgrim’s Bell—Artoria Pendragon.

When she learned of her Proper Human History counterpart, she felt nauseous and that she would never want a life like that. But the forging of Excalibur sealed her counterpart’s fate. Artoria felt if it wasn’t for Excalibur, her counterpart’s life would have been different and Britain would have ended differently. She thought only she could save her every time she heard the bells ring.

But always ever since she was born, an unknown star has watched her amidst the storm. She doesn’t know where she wants to go, doesn’t care about her mission, and she doesn’t want to be the Child of Prophecy. Yet she never stopped moving forward and she never surrendered to the storm. She wants to know why someone like her never gave up and what that star is.

The heroes arrive at the Site of Selection. In its center is the Sky Furnace, where the Fairies of Paradise must return to. Once Artoria offers her body to it, the Holy Sword will be established not only in the Lostbelt, but its concept will be returned to Proper Human History as well. It will not be a temporary recreation either, but the sword itself.

Before she steps into the Sky Furnace, Artoria confesses she hated Britain and its inhabitants, and her mission. She would have preferred living a normal life, but not wanting to disappoint anyone, she acted as the Child of Prophecy. She may have persevered this time, but she is certain she would abandon her mission come a second time, shocked most of all she made it this far.

She never grew fonder of fairies and humans, but she understood everyone had suffered and they all yearned for salvation. She doesn’t think anyone deserves that even if sinners should be punished for their crimes because they are all the same. Beneath all the fake and real lies, though, they all wished to be saved.

Until now, Artoria never thought she could save this world—bring an end to it—because the fate of the world isn’t something anyone should shoulder even if they dislike it. Even so, she will fulfill her mission for her own sake, just like Ritsuka and Mash showed her, for she will never betray that thing amidst the storm.

Stepping into the Sky Furnace, Artoria starts to dissolve. She will lose her memories afterward, followed by her sense of self. The Inner Sea is taking her memories because it is necessary to use the current state of humanity as materials to forge Excalibur. Artoria was merely the vessel to accumulate them to forge a better blade.

Reminiscing, Artoria is certain it will be an excellent blade, a shining star that will not be defeated by any evil on Earth, a sword worthy of her other self. She is glad “Caster” has no spring memories, but for her it is the memory of a wonderful journey.

Now, without form, Artoria accepts her journey has come to an end. But she wanted to know what that star was, knowing she wouldn’t be complete until she did. She thought she’d understand on her journey, but in the end she never found her answer.

Muramasa then suddenly steps into the Sky Furnace and tells her she isn’t done here then, much to Artoria’s confusion. He takes over the forging process and finishes Excalibur, not caring his Spirit Origin was burned up.

Because Muramasa took over her job, Artoria starts being ejected from the Sky Furnace. Muramasa has granted her a small reprieve, so she can finish her unfinished business. He considers it repayment for the others buying his life, even though Artoria tells him that debt was paid long ago.

She tells him he is from the outside world, so he has nothing to do with Britain and reminds him his job is supposed to come afterwards. Muramasa is happy with this outcome, though, because he won’t have to see the Alien God again. He accepts his fate and tells Artoria he wished he could’ve made that hairpiece himself before she is finally ejected.

Mash confirms the disappearance of one Spirit Origin, but to the surprise of everyone, Artoria still lives. Artoria gives Mash the essence of the Holy Sword, believing Chaldea can use it as a base to make their own Divine Construct. She regrets it couldn’t actually be Excalibur.

Everyone is confused how Artoria is still alive when Merlin realizes what Muramasa did for her. Mash confirms Muramasa was the one who disappeared, having sacrificed himself to protect Artoria. Artoria further confirms she is here now because Muramasa was the one who built the final touches on the Holy Sword.

She then explains with the establishment of the Holy Sword, the Lostbelt’s basis has been overturned and it will be pruned before long. But the Great Calamity cannot be allowed to become a pruning phenomenon. It is a threat to humanity that Chaldea must stop themselves.

Ritsuka notices the air about Artoria is different, but they’re glad she is safe and welcomes her back. Artoria responds she is back stronger than ever. Merlin senses she is indeed stronger, and she has gained a new magecraft theory: Holy Sword Creation.

He then decides to rewind the Great Pit’s time to two hours before Cernunnos awoke, suspecting the surface is now overflowing with curses. He thought this might happen, so he looked into the dreams of the Great Pit and made it so the escaped Cernunnos is a “dream” that the god is having and wake him from there. Due to spending all his time after being freed from the Garden casting spells on the Great Pit, his Spirit Origin is worn out. Once Cernunnos’ dream is lifted, the current Merlin will disappear.

Starting to disappear, Merlin decides to send the heroes back to the Storm Border an hour before Cernunnos appeared. He warns they will have another hour to defeat Cernunnos before the curses overflow. He requests Artoria to take care of Ritsuka and Mash from him, calling her king without a name. Artoria affirms she will, knowing Merlin is still imprisoned in his tower even now. Merlin then sends the heroes back.

Merlin realizes he forgot to ask Ritsuka about something. He was watching Chaldea’s journey very closely with his clairvoyance, so he wanted to ask Ritsuka why they were talking with themselves a lot of the time.

INTERLUDE II

Two hours since Barghest became a Calamity, Melusine watched as the Great Calamity unfolded from their air. She understood it was all Aurora’s fault.

It is revealed Aurora had ordered Melusine to massacre the Mirror Clan, calling their prophecies dangerous. She is an amoral narcissistic, whose every action is all for her own self-image, having anyone more popular than her killed. But she possesses no malice whatsoever and truly believed the Mirror Clan was dangerous.

But the Mirror Clan were gentle fairies protecting the remains of Melusine’s original self—Albion. Those women offered their lives to her without resistance. Melusine never wanted to kill them, but did so for Aurora’s sake. She returned to Salisbury on foot rather than flying, hoping Aurora would thank her with a smile.

When she returned, though, she overheard Aurora indirectly implicating her for the Mirror Clan’s demise, calling her a detestable creature who will always be a pale imitation of her. Melusine wailed in the rain, knowing Aurora never loved her. Even so, she still loves Aurora.

In the present, Melusine heads back to Salisbury revealing she will return to her original self should Aurora perish.

Meanwhile, the fairies of Salisbury have become even more violent. While still calling for the genocide of the northern fairies, they now want to kill any fairy infected with Moss and any human reluctant to fight the Moss. They consider Aurora the new queen of Britain and expect her to sacrifice herself to the Moss for them.

Melusine enters Aurora’s room from the bell tower. She hoped they could at least be together for Britain’s end when, to her horror, Aurora suggests they escape. Aurora has concluded the fairies no longer love her and asks Melusine to take her to the outside world. She believes Proper Human History will serve better to create her ideal world, considering humans to be weak. Melusine realizes Aurora will only repeat her actions for the sake of her purpose to be the most beloved of all.

She asks Aurora where Coral is. Aurora recalls she transformed her into a caterpillar for irritating her. She squashed her underfoot a few moments ago.

Melusine declares her unrequited love for Aurora and stabs her, refusing to take her to the outside world. She understands Aurora could never be loved there, as the inhabitants of Proper Human History would see her for the poison she is. Aurora will only deteriorate and suffer in despair in such a world, yet she will continue to delude herself.

Aurora refuses to believe such a future, calling Melusine a mockery of a fairy, and repeats her command. Melusine refuses because she cannot bear to let Aurora suffer. She repeats her declaration of love and stabs Aurora again, knowing the latter’s death means she’ll return to her original state. She collapses immediately afterward and transforms.

A CERTAIN PROPHECY

Goredolf is bewildered that the magical energy in the atmosphere is rising, and a second powerful magical signature has appeared. In addition, Cernunnos has vanished from the Great Pit.

Da Vinci explains magical energy is increasing because of all the fairy corpses being burned up. It won’t cause any immediate concerns, so Da Vinci says just ignore it. The same goes for the second powerful magical signature, which she hasn’t gotten the chance to monitor. As for the Great Pit, she has no idea because all of their measurement data is missing for it. It is as if time itself has rewound only for the Great Pit.'

Suddenly she confirms a space-time ripple in the control room, from which Ritsuka, Mash, and Artoria emerge.'

At Goredolf’s behest, Ritsuka gives a quick report on what happened in Avalon. Afterward, Mash goes to put her shield to the analysis room and Excalibur’s essence in storage at Da Vinci’s request.

With all the information provided by Ritsuka’s report, Holmes declares they can start strategizing now. He requests to Professor to use the ship’s logs regarding the Great Pit to calculate the time between Cernunnos’ appearance and the flood of curses. He also wants both physical and anti-curse defenses prepared since the battle against Cernunnos is liable to be close-range using Servants deployed on the Border’s main deck.

Professor believes they can easily bombard Cernunnos given his size. Holmes replies conventional physical means may not be effective, as Cernunnos’ Divine Core is buried underneath a thick mass of curses. Therefore, the correct approach would be exorcise the curse, which can either be done by Artoria or Cú Chulainn. Only a spiritual attack blessed by those two could possibly provide interference.'

Thinking to himself, however, Holmes notes they are missing the deciding factor and fears they will have to sacrifice the Storm Border.'

Professor agrees with Holmes, stating the Border isn’t equipped for spellcasting. They calculate Cernunnos will appear in 30 minutes and the curses will overflow in another hour.'

Though their greatest enemy is Cernunnos, Holmes warns they cannot allow Barghest to escape Britain. If she does, her existence as a Calamity will be accepted as part of Proper Human History. They must defeat quickly and make preparations for Cernunnos’ appearance. There is no time to spare, so the operation begins immediately.'

Holmes tells Ritsuka, Artoria, Cú Chulainn, Nemo and the Marines to formulate a strategy for defeating Cernunnos. He tells Professor and Da Vinci to work on the Storm Border’s anti-curse barrier when the ship is hit by an attack burned through its armor. Nemo states only a Divine Construct could penetrate the Storm Border’s armor. Da Vinci displays their attack on the monitor.'

The heroes see their attack is a fighter jet-esque dragon flying at supersonic speeds. It comes back for another attack, but thanks to some quick maneuvering, its breath attack only grazes the tip of the Storm Border’s bow. Da Vinci realizes Barghest wasn’t the only one razing Britain and concludes the dragon is Albion, the Calamity of Flame.'

All the Storm Border’s magical resources are spent trying to avoid Albion’s attacks, leaving them vulnerable to the next one. Artoria believes she can stop it as she is now. But she cannot protect the whole ship and it will only be for a few minutes. She can at least protect the ship’s engine with her anti-purge defenses.

Holmes finds that promising but asks Artoria why the Calamities are attacking them when they should be focused on destroying the fairies of Britain. Artoria cannot answer that, so Cú Chulainn answers it is because they obtained the means to save Britain with Artoria returned.'

Understanding Albion’s focus will be on her, Holmes suggests placing Artoria on the bow to lure Albion in for close-quarters combat, jokingly calling it Operation Titanic. Artoria obviously dislikes this plan and says she wouldn’t be able to protect the ship in that situation. She cannot both act as bait and protect the ship.'

Percival offers to act as bait instead so Artoria can focus on protecting the ship. Holmes tells him it wouldn’t work as Albion’s target is Artoria. Percival believes he can at least serve as a substitute since he wields the Spear of Selection, a tool of the Fairies of the Paradise. Not to mention he is an artificial Child of Prophecy.'

A narration describes Albion as a doomsday device meant to incinerate the bodies of fairies possessing none of Melusine’s previous emotions. Its objective is to prevent Britain’s destruction, which is why it seeks to eliminate the Storm Border. But when it sees Percival standing on the ship’s bow, it felt a pain in its heart.'

A flashback shows Percival understood his poor condition meant he would have to leave the fighting to the others.'

But before he headed out onto the deck, he made a final request for Chaldea not to bear any ill will towards Britain and the fairies, understanding that benevolence and malevolence existed in all races and societies.

He accepted Faerie Britain is doomed to perish but believed that did not make a mistake nor was it because it was an evil world. It was an incomplete world, but Chaldea’s world still has a future. Percival thus beseeched them to reach utopia, where all may know salvation.

In the present, the heroes fight Albion after it is successfully lured to the deck by Percival.'

Albion eventually withdraws, but the Storm Border is no condition to pursue. Percival recalls it was Melusine who taught him to devote everything to the one he loves and implores the Spear him to punish for deceiving so many for that reason. He then pierces Albion’s heart with the Spear, his body disintegrating immediately afterward. The pain of seeing her precious little brother perish before her as she falls from the sky restores a modicum of Melusine’s emotions.

Twenty minutes later, it is stated it will take 30 minutes to repair the ship. Cernunnos rises in 10 minutes, so Chaldea will have a grace period of 40 minutes to defeat him before his curses overflow.

The issue, however, is Barghest, who just destroyed Oxford and will soon arrive at Norwich. The Storm Border isn’t capable of eliminating Cernunnos and Barghest both simultaneously. If they wish to eliminate them, the heroes need to split up.

Cú Chulainn advises they focus on Cernunnos since he is a threat is of global proportions. Holmes agrees, saying Barghest can be dealt with in the future even when she becomes a Calamity to Proper Human History once she heads out to sea from Norwich.

But Mash requests separate deployment, not wanting Barghest to become an enemy of Proper Human History. She also thinks it would be too cruel for Barghest to destroy Norwich, hers and Boggart’s hometown. Goredolf allows it and gives her until the Storm Border is ready to head for the Great Pit to complete her objective.

Figuring this would be the case, Holmes confesses he finished the Shadow Border’s maintenance while Artoria’s party descended into the Spirit Cave. Meuniere again agrees to be the driver.

Holmes tells Ritsuka and Mash they only have 30 minutes to eliminate Barghest. Chaldea will catapult the Shadow Border will be catapulted and have it land near Norwich. Once Ritsuka and Mash complete their objective, the Storm Border will receive the Shadow and its personnel at the rendezvous point and head for the Great Pit.

Da Vinci requests to retrieve Tifone, which contains records of their journey, from Mike's tavern in Salisbury. Once the Storm Border launches the Shadow Border, it will head for and standby over Salisbury while Da Vinci retrieves Tifone and Habetrot. Afterward, the Storm Border will head for Norwich's vicinity.

A flashback shows Barghest first met Adonis back in the year 2000 of the Queen’s Calendar when she tried to kill herself with her own horn for once again eating a lover of hers.

Hours before in the present, it is revealed that upon returning to Manchester, Barghest saw the fairies gleefully killing the humans. Unbeknownst to her, they’ve been killing and eating the humans every day in the shed. They have been imitating her ever since they saw her eat Adonis.

Barghest remembered she succumbed to her instincts and ate Adonis. She thought because she only ate the strong, she couldn’t possibly eat him. But she was broke long before then, so strong or weak made no difference.

Out of grief and self-loathing, she tried once again to kill herself. But someone interrupted her and formed a contract with her, considering it too early for her to succumb to her curse. They sealed her memory and made her think Adonis was still alive, telling her to return to his room so she can relive his final days in her dreams.

Hearing the fairies sought to repeat their actions in Proper Human History, Barghest concluded she was mistaken about what to protect for justice never existed in Britain. She then massacred the fairies, realizing they cannot be allowed to leave Britain.

In the present, Barghest destroys the Vault Fortress, killing Spriggan. Arriving near Norwich, Meuniere warns humans with low magic resistance will have their life sucked if they approach Barghest. Ritsuka will therefore have to support Mash from the Shadow. Mash heads out and tries to fight Demon Hound Barghest.

Arriving near Norwich, Meuniere reminds Ritsuka that humans with low magic resistance will have their life sucked if they approach Barghest. They will therefore have to support Mash with remote summoning from inside the Shadow Border with a limit of two Servants. Mash heads out and tries to fight Demon Hound Barghest.

The battle affects Ritsuka adversely when Mash’s shield starts glowing. There is also a new Spirit Origin signature. Ritsuka, recognizing the Spirit Origin, proceeds to inject themselves with an active ampoule before remotely summoning Gawain and Lancelot at Mash’s position.

Though Heroic Spirit summoning should be impossible in this Britain, Gawain posits to Mash it is because their names were engraved on certain individuals. Their Spirit Origins will last a few minutes, but they believe it will suffice. The three Knights of the Round Table then fight Barghest, not to defeat her but to save her.

Barghest is slain, and Gawain and Lancelot disappear instantly. When she was n the verge of death, Barghest had what appeared to be tears in her eyes. It will never be known if they were tears of suffering or tears of gratitude.

Left fatigued by Gawain and Lancelot’s summoning, Mash tries to take 4 minutes to recover her stamina and magical energy to suitable levels when Beryl ambushes her in his Black Wolf. Fortunately, she avoids it thanks to Ritsuka’s warning.

Due to the Moss curse, Beryl is rotting apart, though he immediately regenerates whatever limb rots off.

Beryl recounts how he broke Mash’s fingers in Chaldea’s infirmary to provide her with stimulation through pain back when she had no human emotions. He sees Ritsuka as another obstacle, just like the doctor, and confesses he loves Mash. He has no desire to kill her and wants her to talk with him.

Mash admits Beryl never had hostile intentions toward her. She recounts how he tended to her body when he broke her fingers and listened to her voice intently as she responded to the pain. He said it was love, though he can only express it by inflicting pain.

Ritsuka tells Beryl that isn’t love he is feeling. Beryl tries to argue when the Moss curse progresses even further. He doesn’t seem to mind though, since all it means he will cease being human. So before that happens he will make Mash his bride and attacks Ritsuka for getting in the way of his love.

A flashback shows Beryl as a boy lived in a forest secluded from civilization with his “mother”, a witch with starry eyes. She taught him Witchcraft specifically tailored for assassination, torture, and murder. Beryl killed her as soon as she had nothing left to teach him, crushing her eyes and leaving the forest behind.

Years passed, and he became well know as a mage who specializes in murder. But due to his ill reputation, it became difficult for him to live even in society’s underbelly. Marisbury scouted him when he was looking for a place to belong. Thus, Chaldea’s Antartic base became his third home.

It was there he fell in love with Mash at first sight, wanting to see her grow from an incomplete being. He could only express his love by inflicting pain on her, though, but he still wanted to protect her. He joined Team A on the condition Marisbury would not give Mash a Sirius Light.

Then, a year before Team A’s first Rayshift experiment, Beryl broke into the infirmary and broke Mash’s fingers to express his twisted love for her. He was caught and driven out by the doctor, then detained and forbidden entry afterward.

In the present, on the verge of death following his defeat, Beryl changes back to his human form. He confesses he accepted the Alien God’s resurrection all so he could see Mash again. Ritsuka tries to stop him from getting closer to Mash. Mash allows it though, knowing Beryl can no longer do anything.

Beryl tells Mash he loves her wholeheartedly. Mash accepts that Beryl loves her, but she doesn’t understand his love and doubts anyone ever will. Beryl dies with a smile.

At the same time the Shadow Border was heading for Norwich, Da Vinci was dropped off at Salisbury.

Meanwhile, in his tavern, Mike took note of the turmoil outside. He had known deep inside everyone envied each other, only pretending to be friendly. He thought it was inevitable everyone would turn on each other, as that is the sort of creatures they are.

Though Britain is coming to an end, Mike feared returning to his old boring self more than anything else. The last few days were so fun and special to him, they almost felt like a lie.
