Da Vinci then entered the tavern, fortunate not to have been seen by anyone. Mike was relieved to see her safe, having been worried ever since he heard about the bloody coronation. Da Vinci was glad to see he didn’t turn violent like the other fairies.

Mike told Da Vinci was the kitchen the whole time and ended up devising a recipe for galette. Da Vinci was proud her student came up with it through trial and error. Mike told Da Vinci it was thanks to her he became a full-fledged fairy. Da Vinci considered that an exaggeration, believing Mike would have livened up the tavern eventually even without her.

She then started to head upstair telling Mike she came to pick up Tifone and Habetrot, then immediately return to the Storm Border. The Storm Border is currently parked over Salisbury and once she returns, they’re off to exterminate the Calamity. Da Vinci offered to let Mike tag along, but he declined, saying he would be safe in the basement.

After Da Vinci went upstairs, Mike expressed his fears that Da Vinci’s short lifespan will soon come to an end. Da Vinci then came back down with Tifone and Habetrot in hand. Mike wanted her to stay forever, believing she would live longer that way. He began to turn into a Moss.

Da Vinci thanked Mike for everything and heads for the exit. Seeing her leave, Mike feared he would return to being his old boring self once she was gone. He needed her to stay, again believing she would live longer that way. His transformation progresses further.

But when Da Vinci finally left, Mike dropped the knife he was hiding behind his back. Crying, he realized Da Vinci is only herself because she is free, dazzling because she broadens her horizons and chases her dreams. He suppresses his transformation, concluding he needed to see her off with a smile.

He realized he was wrong for thinking the Fairy Kingdom was a world of dreams because he could get everything and anything. But a “true” dream is unobtainable, and it is what makes living everyday painful yet fulfilling. It’s what makes life worth living, even when the world is ending, even for monsters like fairies. With a smile, Mike bid farewell to Da Vinci, thanking her for coming to his tavern.

With everyone now back together, Chaldea sets course for the Great Pit to begin the operation to eliminate Cernunnos. The Storm Border is fully repaired and combat ready, but 20 minutes have passed since Cernunnos surfaced.

Holmes suspects Cernunnos will use his “arms” to intercept them in response to Artoria’s presence. As it’s impossible to evade such a plethora of curses, Chaldea will need to focus on anti-curse layer defenses and take it all head on.

Cú Chulainn says he will set up a meditative field—a “fountain”—on the deck. Though only a proxy, he is still the god of wisdom, so if he focuses, he can consecrate the entire ship. Holmes states with Cú Chulainn’s assistance, the Storm Border will get close as it can to Cernunnos.

Last time, Cernunnos destroyed Camelot 50 minutes after he surfaced. Chaldea will eliminate him before that happens. Their combatants will be Ritsuka, Artoria, and Mash. Cú Chulainn will be on the deck and if needed, the other Servants will participate as well. The battle cannot go longer than 30 minutes, starting now.

As the Storm Border nears the Great Pit, Ritsuka asks Artoria if she found her reason. Artoria answers she couldn’t find a single convincing reason. But she is fine with that because there seems to be someone who knows the reason why she is trying so hard.

But then she remembers she actually wanted to thank Ritsuka while she had the chance. She informs them she will return to Avalon after Cernunnos is defeated. Calling herself the last remnants of Muramasa’s last work, once she has depleted her magical energy, her role will end and she will enter a deep slumber. She realizes she knew she’d have to return to Avalon ever since she drifted ashore.

However, she wants to repay Ritsuka for taking care of her. While the mission of Fairies of Paradise was to denounce the false Britain, Ritsuka wanted to save it even if it was a fake. For that reason, Artoria wants to share the same goal as them, at least once. As long as they persevere and hold onto hope, she will answer their call. Next time, not to save Britain, but to save the world.

Then out of nowhere Artoria asks Ritsuka what was the most enjoyable part of their journey for them, demanding they leave out Manchester. After Ritsuka gives their answer, Artoria says hers was their third handshake when Ritsuka said they wanted to help the Child of Prophecy. The two then have their fourth and final handshake when Nemo announces they will be engaging Cernunnos in two minutes, so Ritsuka heads to their seat.

In private, Artoria confesses what she enjoyed was Gloucester because while nothing special to Ritsuka, it was the first time in her life she could walk the main street with someone she loved.

Arriving at the Great Pit, Cú Chulainn activates Gambanteinn Valhalla, creating a barrier that exorcises all of Cernunnos’ “arms” approaching the Storm Border. Cernunnos counter-curses him, howver, destroying his left eye. With Ritsuka summoning from the bridge, Chaldea begins their battle to destroy the dead god.

Cernunnos is defeated several times, but his corpse produces curses faster than the heroes can inflict any lasting damage. The heroes cannot purge the Great Calamity unless they attack the source of the curses.

Making a makeshift eyepatch for himself, Cú Chulainn realizes Cernunnos has a Divine Core, despite being dead. He suspects a sacrifice that cursed the fairies had entered the dead god’s Divine Core and resurrected him. Cernunnos’ Divine Core must therefore be destroyed, which can be done with the 12 Rhongomyniads. Fortunately, the heroes already completed their role of ensuring Artoria gets to the throne.

Ritsuka and Mash both realize Artoria is gone from the deck. Now understanding what Artoria meant in their last conversation, Ritsuka tells Mash they’re going on the defensive. They will hold the line until Artoria can destroy Cernunnos with the 12 Rhongomyniads.

Earlier, Artoria had jumped from the Storm Border and landed on Camelot’s rampart. Traversing the castle, now devoid of all life, she makes her way to the throne room. Once there, she will need to understand the mechanisms of Morgan’s throne and readjust for her own use.

In the past, she would have dismissed such a task as an impossible for her. But after returning from the Site of Selection, she is no longer who she once was. More than half of her now exists as a fantasy. She can feel she is now part of the concept of King Arthur, remembering what “Merlin” once told her.

Arriving in the throne room, Artoria looks up at Cernunnos. She thinks his wrath is justified and his curses are righteous. Nonetheless, she disagrees with the Calamities, believing a system is needed to atone for sin, not just punish them. The time for atonement has long past, however.

Artoria connects her consciousness to the throne and launches the 12 Rhongomyniads at Cernunnos. It unfortunately fails to destroy the dead god because of Artoria’s inability to make full use of Morgan’s magecraft. But she refuses to give up and decides to offer up all her remaining power.

Understanding that she is now the “Concept of the Holy Sword”, Artoria switches the magical energy source from Camelot to herself and changes the ritual from Rhongomyniad: Spirit Vein Closing Armament, to Excalibur: Dragon Vein Burning Armament. Then, using her remaining power as the Fairy of Paradise and the magical energy that gives her form, she launches the 12 Rhongomyniads at Cernunnos simultaneously. She disappears immediately afterward.

Artoria’s final attack evaporated 90% of the curses layered on Cernunnos and damaged his body enough to reveal his Divine Core. Unfortunately, he is regenerating and will back to his original state in 360 seconds.

Nemo tells Da Vinci to order the crew to abandon ship, saying to use the Shadow Border. He and the Nemo Series will stay behind and use the Storm Border as a suicide weapon against Cernunnos. Da Vinci declines, however, saying it isn’t necessary as Cernunnos’ Divine Core. She urges Ritsuka to hurry to Mash’s side because their Command Spells will be needed to slay a god.

Mash despairs they lack the means to destroy Cernunnos’ Divine Core before he regenerates. She admonishes herself, believing if she wasn’t afraid back then and didn’t flee her responsibilites, this wouldn’t have happened.

Habetrot appears and tells Mash she running from the fight back then. She knows for all her outward gallantry and courage, Mash is a person who is fundamentally averse to conflict. That hasn’t changed, but she now has the heart and will to fight. For that reason, Habetrot will return to Mash what she took from Sheffield.

A flashback reveals Aesc told Totorot that they are simulations projected by the Fantasy Tree in a “what if” world. Mash never existed in the actual Fairy Calendar, so to avoid a paradox, she will be recorded in history as Galahad and Aesc and Totorot will forget everything about Mash.

Totorot refused to forget Mash, but Aesc warned her they will be erased as paradoxes if they retain their memories of Mash into the Queen’s Calendar. So long as Totorot remembers Mash, she will be someone who shouldn’t exist. The moment Mash is sent away from Norwich, Totorot’s history as the first Fairy Knight will disappear. She will be erased if anyone in the Queen’s Calendar learned the truth.

Because Totorot originated from a Lostbelt—a fantasy—she cannot exist within a Singularity—reality. Aesc therefore decided to erase all of Mash’s memories of her, from when she first met Totorot in Sheffield in the year 2017 of the Queen’s Calendar to the moment she entered the coffin. Totorot would thus be free to act in even the Queen’s Calendar, but it is vital she not interact with Mash under any circumstances because if Mash remembers her she will erased.

In the present, Mash remembers Habetrot/Totorot. Habetrot returns the Black Barrel, which she snatched while Beryl was occupied and kept hidden in her Spinning Wheel, to Mash. But because Mash remembered her, Totorot starts being erased as a paradox.

Totorot accepts her fate, only ever wanting Mash to be happy. Mash meanwhile despairs over Totorot’s fate, wishing she never remembered her. Totorot requests Mash to not forget about her, Aesc, or their journey, which Mash readily agrees to do. She then implores her to finally put Cernunnos to rest, feeling he is in a lot of pain. She urges her to cheer up, wanting to see how awesome she is.

Ritsuka loads their final Command Spell and Mash fires the Black Barrel, destroying Cernunnos’ Divine Core and putting an end to the Great Calamity. She turns to see Totorot’s reaction, but Totorot is now gone. Mash cries over the loss of her friend and thanks her for everything she did for her.

Cú Chulainn understands Ritsuka and Mash are sad over the loss of Artoria and Habetrot. But he believes they were happy to give their all for the ones they believed would win and wanted to see win. He tells Ritsuka and Mash to prioritize that trust over their sadness if they’re going to carry what transpired in their heart because beyond Britain lies the real enemy—the Alien God.

He begins to disappear as he already completed his duty of ensuring Chaldea acquires the Holy Sword. But as a Celtic Heroic Spirit, he was able to see Cerunnnos through to the end. Thanks to that, he saved face as Chaldea’s only ally from Proper Human History.

Mash asks Cú Chulainn if he will be recorded as Caster in Chaldea. Cú Chulainn replies he and even Odin do owe Chaldea, so if his current Spirit Origin is still allowed to him after he returns to the Throne, he will pay Chaldea a visit as a Caster. He disappears, certain they will meet again.

Britain has been saved, but with all the devastation, Mash doubts there are many survivors. She’d like to at least see Britain’s reconstruction through before returning to Chaldea.

Suddenly, Marine reports a gravitational shift has been confirmed across all of Brtiain. Gravity is no longer functioning, and the land is rising as if it’s going to come crashing down. It is a crisis of the highest grade according to SHEBA. The cause is unknown, but in one hour the Earth will be in danger. The Collapse has begun.

Mash is confused by this since all threats to Britain should be gone. Suddenly Oberon appears and reveals there is still a threat, one that was overlooked since it’s been there from the beginning. He reveals the “Insect of the Abyss“ mural was actually a prophecy left behind by Cernunnos’ priestess before she was dismembered, foretelling the planet’s destruction. He notices Blanca has died from absorbing all Britain’s malice aimed at him in his stead, then nonchalantly throws her off the ship.

Seeing Oberon for himself, Holmes is confused by Da Vinci's reports describing him as a Servant from Proper Human History. But Oberon's Spirit Origin isn't that of Proper Human History, nor does he fit into any category of life form. Da Vinci tries to confirm her report, only to find that Oberon's Spirit Origin is fluctuating by the second. It calls into question Oberon's true identity.

Without notice an enormous winged worm-like creature suddenly appears in northern Britain and begins devouring the island. Oberon calls it a conceptual insect, an infinite hole that will consume Britain and the planet. It is also him, though he isn't sure which is the main body. Ritsuka demands Oberon reveal his True Name.

Heeding Ritsuka’s request, Oberon proceeds to change his form. Not only is his appearance different, but so is his Spirit Origin. He expresses disgust with the fact the fairies created a duplicate of the original Britain, which was only lost because of their negligence, using the corpses of Cernunnos and other fairies, along with their way of life. It had sickened the original Britain to the point it sent Calamities to wipe it all out.

Cernunnos was the exception, though, for he was the guardian of the fairies. So long as he existed Oberon couldn't surface. But Morgan had sealed Cernunnos away instead of destroying him. Oberon lacked the means to kill her himself, so he waited for someone who could undo the seal on Cernunnos and defeat him. It was for that reason that he helped Artoria complete her pilgrimage. Ritsuka demands Oberon reveal his name.

Oberon reveals he is a fantasy born from Proper Human History taking the form of the Fairy King Oberon and the embodiment of Britain’s will, which yearned for its own destruction. His name is Oberon Vortigern, the Insect of the Abyss that came to destroy Faerie Britain alongside humanity.

A MIDSUMMER NIGHT'S DREAM

Oberon’s Spirit Origin pattern doesn’t match any know class. Holmes notes heroes are revered while anti-heroes serve as the former’s counterpart, acting as necessary obstacles for the achievement of good. Among them are those who achieve great feats through deception, deceiving even the soul to gain power and achievements greater than the real deal. Holmes thus designates Oberon’s unknown Servant class as Pretender, One who wears a role, a fabricated Heroic Spirit whose enemy is the world itself.

Goredolf asks Holmes what the Insect is. Holmes answers it is likely the concept of a void. But he says it is not a living organism and its “mouth” is actually a hole. Also, while it appears the earth is getting sucked up, it is actually “falling in”.

Holmes uses the analogy of bathtub being drained once its stopper is removed to describe the situation. Right now, Chaldea, or rather space itself, is falling toward the Insect. Escape would be pointless because now with the “stopper” removed, everything on the planet will fall down the hole.

Oberon admits he seeks to destroy humanity out of pure disgust. He reveals Faerie Britain’s destruction required a variety of factors to be fulfilled beyond having Morgan and Chaldea settle things. He only had one chance to get everything right, so he couldn’t afford to be careless. That is why he decided to use Ritsuka as his pawn upon finding them on the beach.

Ritsuka was beyond his expectations, so he would’ve been fine letting them go, but he wants to destroy Chaldea. Vortigern’s purpose was to destroy the false Britain that continued long after its death, meanwhile Oberon’s purpose is to destroy human history. Chaldea, as the defenders of Proper Human History, is thus his enemies. Chaldea then falls into the Insect of the Abyss.

A flashback reveals Vortigern is a doomsday device created by the original Britain itself, born out of its disgust toward living creatures. He was previously the Moss King, but this time was the Servant Oberon. He was summoned amidst the Welsh fairies who all thought he was their king and stood vigil until he had the strength to stand on his own.

Thanks to the influence of Proper Human History brought about by Morgan’s rule, the fairy king now existed in both Proper Human History and Faerie Britain alike. But the Servant Oberon was nothing but lies so everything he says and does becomes warped. As it was his nature as a Heroic Spirit, he couldn’t circumvent it, but he had already planned on deceiving everyone anyhow.

He was summoned the year Ainsel’s Prophecy gained everyone’s attention. He also used what knowledge of the Proper History he possessed to research the story of King Arthur, Morgan’s archenemy, all so he could manipulate the new Fairy of Paradise.

It is further revealed Oberon’s first sight was the Welsh fairies on and around him. He harbored no love for the creatures living on Faerie Britain, but his purpose of destroying the island also depressed him. Nevertheless, he had no choice but to fulfill his function. But he was too weak to move or even close his eyes and so spent half a year swarmed by the Welsh fairies.

Being depended on and crowned king by those who were deceived countless times disgusted Oberon. But above all else, he was disgusted with himself as the one they flocked to. His hatred and repulsion wasn’t directed toward anyone in particular, though, but the structure of the world itself.

A voice calls out to Ritsuka, telling them to get ahold of themselves. They’re certain Ritsuka will show them an amazing comeback at the very end.

Ritsuka wakes up in the Abyss and sees a single star shining in the distance. Oberon is surprised to see them alive and awake and surmises it is because they overcame their dejections. He says it will only to lead a more painful death though.

Ritsuka demands to know where Mash is. Oberon answers she is sleeping right next to them, though she lacks form because she hasn’t awakened yet. That includes everyone else on the ship.

Describing the Insect of the Abyss as an infinite void, Oberon explains everything swallowed by it will continue to fall for eternity. It is impossible to escape from it unless he is killed. But everyone is in the midst of a “falling dream” and unless they wake up like Ritsuka did, they will not appear.

Oberon gives Ritsuka the choice of going to sleep or die fighting him to try to save the world. Ritsuka chooses to fight, believing a chance at victory is on its way. The distant star then shines more brightly as Ritsuka calls forth Caster. Artoria arrives in a new form in response to Ritsuka’s summons in accordance with their contract.

A flashback reveals after losing her form, Artoria was trapped amidst the storm of malice surrounded by voices cursing her for fulfilling her duty. The star was no longer there, having never existed. Artoria concluded simply never wanting to betray that star meant she could never save anyone beside herself. She was ready to accept her fate of disappearing forever when someone from long ago reached out to her——the blue-haired fairy.

Her name was Hope, and she had grown tired of her role of giving hope to everyone and lamented, yet she never lost her smile. She protected and believed in Artoria all this time simply because Artoria randomly offered to give her her name. It may seem to be a trivial reason to others, but Artoria thanked her for cherishing it.

Artoria realized she never had an exemplary reason, either. She simply doesn’t want to betray that star nor discard this feeling inside her. She doesn’t care if it seems like an insignificant reason to others. It is the only thing she can believe in and that alone keeps her going even now.

Oberon is surprised to see Artoria became a Heroic Spirit, which was essentially what he told her to become. He doesn’t know if she is her future self or if she is a guardian, but he asserts she has already lost since Britain has perished. As she has no business coming back.

Artoria concedes Oberon is correct for saying that which she protects is gone. She also admits he was right to destroy Faerie Britian, agreeing it is unsightly for which is dead to continue clinging onto life. But she cannot allow him to destroy Proper Human History, for it still has a future.

Mash then wakes up, thereby regaining her form. Oberon realizes it’s Artoria’s doing and concludes everyone else is awake as well. Artoria asks for Mash’s assistance in destroying the source of the Insect of the Abyss before it can propagate beyond Britain. Oberon accepts the challenge, having grown tired of talking, and permits the usage of summoning.

Ritsuka asks Oberon one last time what his objective is. Oberon answers he wants to eliminate both humans and fairies for being equally disgusting, which is why he used Ritsuka. Everything he says is a lie, so he never cared about Britain’s peace, Artoria’s mission, or anything else. Ritsuka admits that is all true, but asks what about Titania to Oberon’s chagrin.

Oberon announces his True Name again, proclaiming himself a Threat to Humanity, a doomsday device of deception. But unlike the Beasts, he will never harbor any love and he shall prove it with this battle. He then commences the final battle of the Lost World, declaring that the pilgrimage ends here.

Ritsuka falls asleep amid the battle and encounters Oberon in a dream of the Forest of Wales. Asked by Ritsuka for his true feelings, Oberon admits he despises Proper Human History for rejecting fantasy as wrong and meaningless. He believes those fantasies have the right to continue to exist, and thus, he sought to destroy humanity. He then urges Ritsuka to wake up.

Artoria and Mash defeat Oberon, plummeting him down into the Abyss. Artoria states Oberon will never come back up with the wound inflicted by her Holy Sword (it is both food and poison for him), and thus he will fall for all eternity.

She starts to disappear, which is to be expected since she came of her own will, similar to what Merlin did. Nemo contacts Ritsuka and warns the Insect is currently losing its connection to the outside world. Chaldea must escape before that happens, so Nemo implores Ritsuka and Mash to get back inside immediately.

Artoria reveals she isn’t Artoria Caster. The latter’s existence is unique to the Lostbelt and she will return to the Inner Sea, along with the shadow of Paradise. However, her actions and the answers she found are engraved in Artoria.

She is not King Arthur, but rather the keeper of the Holy Sword—the conceptual manifestation of the “Knight of the Holy Sword”. Only those chosen by the Holy Sword—those who wield—are entrusted with it. But Artoria made an exception for Ritsuka because of the contract they established with her other self.

Insect’s collapse progresses, so Artoria implores Ritsuka and Mash to hurry and escape. She tells them goodbye and disappears, saying to go without fear, for the light of the stargazers of Chaldea will illuminate their path. Ritsuka bids farewell to Artoria, calling her their friend and kindred spirit.

Ritsuka and Mash return to the Storm Border’s control room. Unfortunately, Chaldea finds it impossible to escape the Insect from within. But suddenly a mysterious explosion occurs 3000 km away, creating an exit to the outside. For whatever reason Albion had attacked the Insect with her heat ray.

Earlier, as she soared through the sky and her body was crumbling away, Albion reviewed fragments of her fading memory. She then encountered the Insect, referring to it as the White Dragon of Britain and her kin. Immediately, she rushed toward it, accepting her body would self-destruct.

She also accepted she was an outsider to Faerie Britain from the beginning. She never had land or peace. All she desired was the boundary that divides heaven and earth. But she had gained something, even if it was false, even if she had to let it go. She forgot what it was, but the void in her memories proved it existed. Declaring herself Fairy Knight, she launched her final attack at the Insect, letting out one last roar.

The roar prompted Aurora to open her eyes, who was still bleeding out from when Melusine stabbed her. She seemed obvious to the fact she was the only one left alive in Salisbury.

She recalled a conversation she had with Oberon, in which it was revealed she had caused the fall of Londinium in the Fairy Calendar through her followers. She also knew beforehand Barghest would be at the farm. Oberon didn’t mind Barghest meeting Artoria, though, since it advanced his schedule. It is also confirmed Spriggran was another of Oberon’s collaborators.

Oberon warned Aurora to refrain from laying a hand on Artoria and Ritsuka as they’re important pawns of his for defeating Morgan. Aurora questioned if she can trust Oberon’s purpose is Morgan’s downfall. Oberon lied he wanted to secure Britain’s future, telling Aurora he believed her to be the most suitable ruler for Britain.

However, he affirmed he cannot be trusted too much as his nature as a Heroic Spirit means everything he says becomes a lie. He called Aurora his polar opposite as everything she says becomes the truth, with everyone, including herself, believing in her lies. Aurora is unaware she is lying, though. She only survived for so long because she is both perpetrator and bystander. She is not the most beautiful fairy in Britain, but the most innocent usurper.

Returning to the relative present, Aurora wished she had captured and killed Oberon.

Everything had done for the past 3000 years was to make her “shine”. Once, however, she did something that wasn’t for benefit when she visited the Lake District. She picked up the lump of flesh that would become Melusine for looking good in front of her attendants. An act meant to prove by saving such a repulsive thing she was more wonderful than anyone. To her surprise, the lump of flesh sincerely wept and decided it wanted to be like Aurora, becoming a beautiful fairy.

Though immortal, Aurora aged all the same and the brilliance of her wings diminished since the Queen’s Calendar began. That brilliance would continue to fade unless she kept herself entertained with new and unusual things.

Melusine, meanwhile, remained strong and beautiful, which is why, for the sake of her pride, Aurora made her suffer. Yet Melusine continued to protect Aurora and killed her, not for her own sake, but Aurora’s. She knew Aurora didn’t fear death, but losing her brilliance.

Aurora admitted she hated Melusine and was relieved they parted ways. She had only embraced the lump of flesh (like a reflection of her own ugliness) for appearance’s sake. But watching Albion’s final flight, she smiled for the first time, knowing she did something good, and died.

In the present, Chaldea uses the exit Albion had them and escapes the Lost World.

Oberon, falling endlessly, hatefully saw them off, delighted he accomplished his goal of destroying Britain. His only regret was that he couldn’t fulfill his wish of destroying Proper Human History for Titania’s sake. Then, as the Abyss closes, he sees the sky of Proper Human History, calling it both beautiful and disgusting.

EPILOGUE

Chaldea returns safely to Proper Human History. Goredolf congratulates everyone on a successful mission. Nemo says the ship is currently cruising at low speed for a system check and an airnet has been deployed at the edge of the deck. He grants permission to head out onto the deck for 20 minutes so those involved may look and bring closure to their memories of Britain.

Ritsuka and Mash head out onto the deck. They both lament the Fairy Kingdom is gone, but Ritsuka remarks it still existed, just like all the other Lostbelts. Mash agrees, considering the Lostbelts precious memories of a journey that taught many precious lessons.

The pair are joined by Da Vinci, who pours one out for Mike. She believes and hopes Mike is still working at his tavern in a different pruned world. But she notes what once was will always remain in the planet’s memories. She vows to cross that boundary and pay Mike a visit to see how he’s progressed.

Changing the subject, Da Vinci tells Ritsuka and Mash to hurry to her workshop. A Spirit Origin has started glowing, and it is uncertain who will be summoned. Mash, feeling potential danger, says to leave it to her, believing she is still “Super Mash”. Ritsuka informs her, though, that her Spirit Origin output has returned to normal. Leaving that aside, Mash suggests they head for Da Vinci’s workshop, feeling a wonderful encounter awaits them. Ritsuka, Mash, and Da Vinci head there their memories of Britain engraved in the hearts.

Meanwhile, in the Wandering Sea, Sion congratulates Chaldea on preventing the future of the Collapses with two minutes left. Afterward, she looks over the remaining special dangerous cases: Koyanskaya and the possible formation of the largest Singularity ever recorded.

As for Kadoc’s recovery, Sion couldn’t extract Sirius Light, so she thinks they’ll have to give up on that front.

The hieroglyphic predictions say “No problem. Rebel. Eyelids.” Sion has no idea what it means, so she decides to ignore it as well, thinking their attention should be on the Alien God’s next move. There is also another prediction, but Sion calls it obvious and ignores as there isn’t much time.

She suspects the Alien God has finished her studying now that Chaldea has conquered six Lostbelts. Both sides have narrowed their targets down to one, so safety is no longer assured from this point onward.

Non-Servants
